```python
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def closestCost(self, baseCosts: List[int], toppingCosts: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        toppingCosts *= 2

        @lru_cache(None)
        def fn(i: int, x: int) -> int:
            """Return sum of subsequence of toppingCosts[i:] closest to x."""
            if x < 0 or i == len(toppingCosts):
                return 0

            skip = fn(i + 1, x)
            take = toppingCosts[i] + fn(i + 1, x - toppingCosts[i])
            return min(skip, take, key=lambda y: (abs(y - x), y))

        ans = float('inf')
        for bc in baseCosts:
            ans = min(ans, bc + fn(0, target - bc), key=lambda x: (abs(x - target), x))
        return ans
```

Need maybe include `@lru_cache(None)` but if using type hints with inner function, `List` import. Let's write final code:

```python
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def closestCost(self, baseCosts: List[int], toppingCosts: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        toppingCosts *= 2

        @lru_cache(None)
        def fn(i: int, x: int) -> int:
            if x < 0 or i == len(toppingCosts):
                return 0

            skip = fn(i + 1, x)
            take = toppingCosts[i] + fn(i + 1, x - toppingCosts[i])
            return min(skip, take, key=lambda y: (abs(y - x), y))

        ans = float('inf')
        for bc in baseCosts:
            ans = min(ans, bc + fn(0, target - bc), key=lambda z: (abs(z - target), z))
        return ans
```

Need consider if `x < 0` base case before `i == len(toppingCosts)` could cause index? No.

Wait, original toppingCosts list represents distinct topping types, even if same cost. If there are two topping types both cost 1, each can be used 0,1,2 times, so total copies of cost 1 can be 0..4. Duplicating the list [1,1] -> [1,1,1,1] and choosing any subset allows up to 4 copies of cost 1, which is correct because there are two types each with two copies. Good.

Let's ensure no syntax errors:
```
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def closestCost(self, baseCosts: List[int], toppingCosts: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        toppingCosts *= 2

        @lru_cache(None)
        def fn(i: int, x: int) -> int:
            if x < 0 or i == len(toppingCosts):
                return 0

            skip = fn(i + 1, x)
            take = toppingCosts[i] + fn(i + 1, x - toppingCosts[i])
            return min(skip, take, key=lambda y: (abs(y - x), y))

        ans = float('inf')
        for bc in baseCosts:
            ans = min(ans, bc + fn(0, target - bc), key=lambda z: (abs(z - target), z))
        return ans
```
Syntax okay. The inner function uses `toppingCosts` from enclosing scope; no assignment to `toppingCosts` inside, so okay.

The recursion will consider both paths, but they yield same sum. Memoization may not combine because different i? Actually skip i then take i+1: at i skip -> state (i+1, x); at i+1 take -> state (i+2, x-c). Take i then skip i+1: at i take -> state (i+1, x-c); at i+1 skip -> state (i+2, x-c). Both can lead to same later state but with different x? Wait after taking one copy, x is reduced by c. After skipping first and taking second, x is reduced by c at i+1. Both end at i+2 with same x-c. So duplicate paths, but memoization handles. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
final. I've been overthinking. Need output code only.

Let's final with raw code.</think>```python
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def closestCost(self, baseCosts: List[int], toppingCosts: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        toppingCosts *= 2

        @lru_cache(None)
        def fn(i: int, x: int) -> int:
            if x < 0 or i == len(toppingCosts):
                return 0

            skip = fn(i + 1, x)
            take = toppingCosts[i] + fn(i + 1, x - toppingCosts[i])
            return min(skip, take, key=lambda y: (abs(y - x), y))

        ans = float('inf')
        for bc in baseCosts:
            ans = min(ans, bc + fn(0, target - bc), key=lambda z: (abs(z - target), z))
        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
from functools import lru_cache

class Solution:
    def closestCost(self, baseCosts: List[int], toppingCosts: List[int], target: int) -> int:
        toppingCosts *= 2

        @lru_cache(None)
        def fn(i: int, x: int) -> int:
            if x < 0 or i == len(toppingCosts):
                return 0

            skip = fn(i + 1, x)
            take = toppingCosts[i] + fn(i + 1, x - toppingCosts[i])
            return min(skip, take, key=lambda y: (abs(y - x), y))

        ans = float('inf')
        for bc in baseCosts:
            ans = min(ans, bc + fn(0, target - bc), key=lambda z: (abs(z - target), z))
        return ans
